Blackmun’s Opinion of the Court, this proposition; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority support at the stated level of generality; no Marks aggregation.
- Reliable scientific foundation and fit require flexible preliminary screening: Daubert v. Merrell Dow Pharmaceuticals — Reliable scientific foundation and fit require flexible preliminary screening, 1993-06-28. Blackmun’s Opinion of the Court, this proposition; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (8 Justices). Direct majority support at the stated level of generality; no Marks aggregation.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-06-28; Daubert v. Merrell Dow Pharmaceuticals, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** Daubert v. Merrell Dow Pharmaceuticals, 509 U.S. 579 (1993).

## Source: state/STANDARDS_AND_TESTS.md — Late assertion of Teague nonretroactivity

### Late assertion of Teague nonretroactivity

**Governing question:** Whether an omitted Teague defense must be entertained when first raised in Supreme Court merits briefing.

**Current rule:** Teague nonretroactivity is not jurisdictional. When the State omitted it below and in its certiorari opposition and first asserted it in merits briefing, the Court may decline consideration. The permission is discretionary, not a mandatory waiver rule. A consistently preserved subsidiary defense limiting the very constitutional rule and habeas relief under review presents the distinct fair-inclusion setting addressed in Caspari; preservation and fair inclusion remain separate.

**Authority by component:**

- Discretion to decline the late nonjurisdictional defense: Schiro v. Farley — Late presentation of nonretroactivity, 1994-01-19. O'Connor's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg, seven Justices. Blackmun and Stevens also agree with merits reach, but their agreement is unnecessary to the opinion's controlling authority.
- Preserved subsidiary-defense distinction: Caspari v. Bohlen — The preserved nonretroactivity defense is fairly included here, 1994-02-23. O'Connor's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; eight Justices. Stevens disagrees with fair inclusion and would decline the defense under Rule 14.1(a).

**Present operation effective:** Schiro v. Farley, 1994-01-19, for discretionary treatment of the late defense. Caspari, 1994-02-23, supplies the distinct preserved-defense comparison, not a later change to that discretion.

**Related current rules:** “Presented questions, subsidiary arguments and exceptional Supreme Court review” supplies the question-presentation boundary. Neither decision changes Teague’s new-rule inquiry or either contemporary exception.

**Holdings navigation:** Late presentation of nonretroactivity; The preserved nonretroactivity defense is fairly included here.

## Source: state/STANDARDS_AND_TESTS.md — Capital habeas counsel before a merits petition and discretionary stays

### Capital habeas counsel before a merits petition and discretionary stays

**Governing question:** When an indigent capital prisoner may obtain qualified counsel and a stay while preparing federal habeas review.

**Current rule:** A defendant financially unable to obtain adequate representation or reasonably necessary services is entitled to commence the §848(q)(4)(B) representation proceeding before filing the merits application by requesting counsel to prepare an identified §2254 or §2255 challenge seeking to vacate or set aside a death sentence. For this postjudgment appointment, at least one attorney must ordinarily have five years’ admission to the relevant court of appeals and three years’ experience handling felony appeals there. For good cause the court may appoint another attorney whose background, knowledge or experience permits proper representation, considering the penalty’s seriousness and the litigation’s unique and complex nature. Under amended §848(q)(9), upon a finding that investigative, expert or other services are reasonably necessary for representation concerning guilt or sentence, the court may authorize attorneys to obtain them and, if authorized, must order fees and expenses paid under paragraph (10). Ex parte proceedings, communications or requests cannot be considered without a proper showing of need for confidentiality; any such matter must be transcribed and made part of the appellate-review record. Appointment alone establishes no entitlement to unidentified expenses.

**Authority by component:**

- Prepetition representation and statutory qualifications: McFarland v. Scott — Qualified capital counsel may be appointed before the merits application, 1994-06-30. Part I of Justice Stevens's opinion, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Blackmun, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter and Ginsburg. Seven Justices directly adopt the statutory rule and its application to the October 22 motion.
- Pending preparation proceeding and express anti-injunction exception: McFarland v. Scott — The statutory preparation proceeding satisfies §2251 pendency; Section 2251 supplies the express statutory exception to §2283, 1994-06-30. Part II of Justice Stevens's opinion, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Blackmun, Kennedy, Souter and Ginsburg. Six Justices adopt this construction; O'Connor does not join it. Part III of Justice Stevens's opinion, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Blackmun, Kennedy, Souter and Ginsburg. Six Justices directly adopt this statutory conclusion; it is distinct from the appointment entitlement and from the exercise of stay discretion.
- Reasonable preparation and individualized stay discretion: McFarland v. Scott — Reasonable preparation and individualized stay discretion govern the remand, 1994-06-30. Part IV of Justice Stevens's opinion, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Blackmun, Kennedy, Souter and Ginsburg. Six adopt the stay-discretion rule and common remand; O'Connor separately joins the appointment direction under Part I only.

- Discretionary services authorization, mandatory payment once authorized and confidentiality/transcription conditions: Source, authority and operative text, Public Law 104-132, Title I, §108, amended 21 U.S.C. §848(q)(9), enacted 1996-04-24; the Record’s retained complete enacted text supplies these components.

**Present operation effective:** Services provision amended by AEDPA, 1996-04-24; McFarland’s counsel, pendency and stay components remain effective from 1994-06-30.

**Sequence or decisionmaker:** Representation continues through available judicial and postconviction proceedings, stays and appropriate motions, and available competency and clemency proceedings unless similarly qualified counsel replaces the attorney on the attorney’s or defendant’s motion; the full reach into later state proceedings remains open. The statutory preparation proceeding is pending for §2251 purposes. The judge before whom it is pending may stay state action against the detained person concerning a matter involved in the habeas proceeding, before final judgment, after final judgment of discharge or pending appeal. Covered state action after a stay is void; without a stay, it remains valid. Section 2251 supplies §2283’s express statutory exception, without finding its other exceptions satisfied. A stay is discretionary, not automatic or indefinite. The prisoner must receive a reasonable preparation opportunity, while proved inexcusable disregard of that opportunity and obstruction can justify denial. The distinct prejudgment counsel qualification—five years in the trial-court bar and three years’ actual felony-trial experience there—does not replace the postjudgment standard.

**Holdings navigation:** Qualified capital counsel may be appointed before the merits application; The statutory preparation proceeding satisfies §2251 pendency; Section 2251 supplies the express statutory exception to §2283; Reasonable preparation and individualized stay discretion govern the remand. Lindh v. Murphy (1997-06-23) — The chapter 153 amendments at issue do not govern this pre-enactment noncapital application.



**Effective transition:** Enacted April 24, 1996. “Temporal reach of ordinary AEDPA habeas amendments” states Lindh's noncapital chapter 153 application-date construction, effective 1997-06-23. Separately directed provisions, chapter 154's express pending-case clause and Felker's successive-petition treatment remain distinct; Lindh does not settle every other Title I provision's application to pending proceedings. Authority: Lindh v. Murphy — The chapter 153 amendments at issue do not govern this pre-enactment noncapital application, Ginsburg's six-Justice opinion, 1997-06-23.

**Former-rule note:** In proceedings still governed by former §848(q)(9), a reasonably necessary-services finding required authorization and payment at reasonably necessary rates/amounts notwithstanding general criminal-case limits, with ex parte consideration; retroactive authorization/payment was permitted if timely procurement could not practicably await approval. The replacement text changes authorization to “may,” directs payment under paragraph (10) and adds the confidentiality/transcription conditions; it does not decide every pending-case transition.

## Source: state/STANDARDS_AND_TESTS.md — Independent Miranda custody review under the former habeas statute

### Independent Miranda custody review under the former habeas statute

**Governing question:** Classification of ultimate Miranda custody and historical facts in proceedings governed by former §2254(d).

**Current rule:** Whether established interrogation circumstances would make a reasonable person understand freedom of movement as restrained to the degree associated with formal arrest is a mixed constitutional question requiring independent federal habeas review. Former §2254(d)’s factual presumption does not attach to that ultimate conclusion. Historical events and supported credibility findings retain statutory protection. A competent state court’s factual determination after a merits hearing involving the applicant and State or its officer or agent, evidenced by written findings, opinion or other reliable adequate written indicia, is presumed correct subject to the statutory exceptions. Those exceptions are an unresolved factual dispute; factfinding procedure lacking a full and fair hearing; inadequately developed material facts; absence of jurisdiction over subject matter or applicant; unconstitutional denial of counsel to an indigent applicant; denial of a full, fair and adequate hearing; other denial of due process; or a pertinent produced state record, as a whole, failing fairly to support the determination. At a federal evidentiary hearing, after due proof of the state determination, the applicant must establish error by convincing evidence unless one of the first seven circumstances is established, otherwise appears or is admitted, or the federal court finds the eighth. A ruling on papers does not itself establish an exception or require a fresh hearing.

**Authority by component:**

- Ultimate classification: Ultimate Miranda custody requires independent federal review; factual conditions and exceptions: The statutory protection of historical factfinding remains unchanged, 1995-11-29. O’Connor joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer (eight).

**Present operation effective:** Thompson, 1995-11-29.

**Effective transition:** This entry states the former §2254(d) regime. AEDPA separately enacted §§2254(d) and (e) on April 24, 1996. Lindh preserves former law for the covered noncapital applications filed before April 24, 1996; see “Temporal reach of ordinary AEDPA habeas amendments” and Lindh v. Murphy — The chapter 153 amendments at issue do not govern this pre-enactment noncapital application (1997-06-23). Separately directed provisions remain distinct; Thompson does not displace a genuinely applicable new statutory restriction.

**Related current rules:** “Communicated circumstances and Miranda custody” supplies the substantive custody inquiry; “State-court merits decisions and factual development under amended §2254” supplies the distinct amended text.

**Holdings navigation:** Thompson v. Keohane, 516 U.S. 99. Lindh v. Murphy (1997-06-23) — The chapter 153 amendments at issue do not govern this pre-enactment noncapital application.

## Source: state/STANDARDS_AND_TESTS.md — Independent appellate review of suspicion and nonwarrant probable cause

### Independent appellate review of suspicion and nonwarrant probable cause

**Governing question:** Appellate review of ultimate reasonable suspicion and probable cause after a nonwarrant search.

**Current rule:** Appellate courts independently decide whether established circumstances, objectively considered in their totality, supply reasonable suspicion for the stop and probable cause for the nonwarrant search. Ultimate sufficiency needs no clear-error finding. Historical facts, credibility and genuinely factual subsidiary findings or inferences receive clear-error review. Grounded contextual inferences of resident judges and experienced local officers receive genuine due weight, without controlling ultimate constitutional sufficiency. Experience does not establish an unproved observation, and no police-credibility presumption, numerical weight or intermediate standard follows.

**Authority by component:**

- Review allocation, objective totality and due weight: II. Ultimate constitutional sufficiency is independently reviewed, with factual protection and genuine due weight, 1996-05-28; O’Connor with Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer (eight).

**Present operation effective:** Ornelas, 1996-05-28.

**Exceptions and limits:** Gates’s substantial-basis review of an issuing magistrate remains distinct. Not every mixed question is independently reviewed. The rule creates no new hearing, remedy, preservation exception or immediate conditional-plea withdrawal right.

**Holdings navigation:** Ornelas v. United States, 517 U.S. 690.

## Source: state/STANDARDS_AND_TESTS.md — Objective probable cause for an ordinary initial traffic stop

### Objective probable cause for an ordinary initial traffic stop

**Governing question:** An ordinary initial traffic stop supported by objective probable cause despite another investigative purpose.

**Current rule:** Objective probable cause of a traffic violation supports an ordinary initial stop notwithstanding an unrelated investigative aim. Validity does not additionally depend on whether reasonable officers ordinarily would enforce that violation absent that aim, or on compliance with customary assignment and enforcement policies. Manner, scope and duration remain independently constrained: a materially greater detention or search requires independent grounds. This rule does not remove purpose-specific conditions from inventory and administrative exceptions; inventories retain standardized criteria or established routine and genuine inventory purposes rather than investigatory rummaging. Purposeful racial selection remains independently prohibited by Equal Protection.

**Authority by component:**

- Objective initial-stop rule: Objective traffic probable cause supports this ordinary initial stop; independent execution and discrimination limits: Policy, execution and discrimination limits, 1996-06-10; Thomas’s unanimous opinion.

**Present operation effective:** Whren, 1996-06-10.

**Open question:** No discovery entitlement, immunity, suppression consequence or damages remedy for discriminatory selection is decided. Armstrong’s federal-charging threshold is not transferred to stop claims; traffic probable cause does not authorize every later intrusion.

**Holdings navigation:** Whren v. United States, 517 U.S. 806.

## Source: state/STANDARDS_AND_TESTS.md — Confidential psychotherapy communications under Rule 501

### Confidential psychotherapy communications under Rule 501

**Governing question:** Federal common-law protection for confidential treatment communications with licensed psychotherapists.

**Current rule:** Confidential patient communications to a licensed psychotherapist in diagnosis or treatment, including actual confidential psychotherapy by a licensed clinical social worker, are privileged under federal common law. The protection covers treatment conversations and counseling notes, but title alone does not protect administrative exchanges, ordinary workplace counseling or communications outside confidential treatment. Protection does not turn on routine lawsuit-specific balancing of the opponent’s evidentiary need; a valid invocation cannot justify an adverse inference or instruction treating withholding as legally unjustified. Waiver remains possible, but no particular waiver rule is established.

**Authority by component:**

- Protected relationship and social-worker inclusion: I. Confidential psychotherapy communications are privileged; no routine balancing and no adverse inference: II. Protection does not depend on routine balancing in each lawsuit, 1996-06-13. Stevens with Stone-Zsela, O’Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer (eight) on each holding.

**Present operation effective:** Jaffee, 1996-06-13.

**Exceptions and limits:** Rule 501’s Constitution, congressional and authorized-rule boundaries and its separate state-law sentence remain. No general physician-patient privilege follows. Conflict for evidence relevant to federal and state claims, court-ordered examinations, litigation use, constitutional criminal overrides and serious threats remain unadjudicated. The seven-Justice serious-threat illustration is not an operative exception.

**Holdings navigation:** Jaffee v. Redmond, 518 U.S. 1.

## Source: state/STANDARDS_AND_TESTS.md — Criminal fair warning under §242

### Criminal fair warning under §242

**Governing question:** Whether pre-existing law gave adequate warning that an official's charged conduct was constitutionally unlawful under 18 U.S.C. §242.

**Current rule:** The statute alone or as authoritatively construed must make the particular constitutional unlawfulness reasonably clear when the defendant acts; novel or unforeseeable criminal applications are forbidden. Relevant pre-existing law may include lower-court authority, without a Supreme-Court-only requirement, automatic sufficiency of a single lower decision or fixed numerical consensus. An obviously covered application needs no factually matching precedent, but abstract invocation of liberty or bodily security does not settle an actually unresolved application. Criminal fair warning and civil clearly-established-law analysis use the same legal-clarity threshold.

**Authority by component:**

- United States v. Lanier — Fair warning turns on the law when the official acted, 1997-03-31; Stone-Zsela for all nine supplies conduct-time notice and the limit on reliance on bad purpose
- United States v. Lanier — Relevant authority is not confined to Supreme Court decisions, 1997-03-31; the same all-nine coalition supplies the permissible legal landscape
- United States v. Lanier — Obvious applications need no factual twin, 1997-03-31; the same all-nine coalition rejects a factual-twin requirement while preserving unsettled boundaries
- United States v. Lanier — The same clarity protection leaves criminal elements intact, 1997-03-31; the same all-nine coalition identifies the shared clarity protection without merging criminal liability

**Present operation effective:** Lanier, 1997-03-31.

**Exceptions and limits:** Willful bad purpose cannot cure an unknowable constitutional prohibition. The Government must still establish constitutional deprivation, action under color of law, criminal willfulness and every other element under the criminal burden. Due-process rights and direct official assaults outside custody are not categorically excluded, but no particular count's merits or notice is predetermined.

**Related current rules:** Relevant precedent in appellate qualified-immunity review retains its civil review, timing and preservation mechanics.

**Holdings navigation:** United States v. Lanier (1997-03-31) — Criminal Procedure / Criminal civil-rights liability and fair warning.

## Source: state/STANDARDS_AND_TESTS.md — Cable access permissions and the invalid compulsory segregation system

### Cable access permissions and the invalid compulsory segregation system

**Governing question:** The surviving 1992 §10 access provisions, their constitutional limits, and the distinct 1996 §506 permissions.

**Current rule:** 1992 §10(a) remains operative independently of §10(b): on leased-access capacity, an operator may prospectively enforce a written and published policy barring programming reasonably believed to describe or depict sexual or excretory activities or organs in a patently offensive manner under contemporary community standards, with FCC standards for the cable medium. It permits rather than commands exclusion. For covered leased-access programming not voluntarily prohibited under (a), §10(b)'s integral compulsory identification/notification, channel-segregation, default-blocking and advance written-election arrangement may not be imposed on willing speakers and adult recipients; its cumulative burdens lack adequate justification. Section 10(c) and its rules may not authorize suppression of protected nonobscene indecency on public, educational and governmental access capacity. The decision does not invalidate lawful obscenity controls or independently adjudicate §10(c)'s category for material soliciting or promoting unlawful conduct, construed by the FCC as otherwise proscribed material.

Separately, 1996 §506(a) permits refusal of public-access programming or portions containing obscenity, indecency or nudity; §506(b) permits refusal of leased-access programming or portions containing those categories. These permissions neither command refusal nor incorporate the 1992 written-policy, reasonable-belief or compulsory subscriber mechanisms. Public-access wording does not itself include all educational and governmental capacity.

**Authority by component:**

- Section 10(a) permission and severability: 1. Section 10(a): particular permission upheld; divided sufficient grounds and 4. Severability and the scope of relief, 1996-06-28; six support retention (Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Souter, Thomas, Breyer), but only four support its balancing rationale.
- Invalid integral §10(b) arrangement: 2. Section 10(b): the integral compulsory design violates the First Amendment, 1996-06-28; Souter Part III, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O’Connor, Kennedy, Ginsburg and Breyer (seven).
- Limited §10(c) invalidity: 3. Section 10(c): protected-indecency authority invalidated; reasons remain divided, 1996-06-28; Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer (six judgment supporters), divided between two three-Justice sufficient grounds.
- Independent 1996 permissions: Public Law 104-104 §506(a)–(b), amending 47 U.S.C. §§531(e), 532(c)(2), effective 1996-02-08; the same distinct source boundary is preserved by Denver, all nine, on 1996-06-28.

**Present operation effective:** Denver Area/Alliance, 1996-06-28, for the 1992 provisions; the separate §506 text operates from 1996-02-08.

**Open question:** No majority adopts a complete general framework for access permissions, a universal public-forum classification, or a rule making every operator choice governmental. Section 506’s constitutionality and particular exercises remain unadjudicated; §10(c)’s invalidation does not extinguish that independent authority or establish unconditional carriage rights.

**Exceptions and limits:** The §10(b) holding addresses its integral machinery, not every notification, age screen, voluntary request or blocking device. The statutory thirty days was an outside fulfillment period, not a mandatory wait. No categorical serious-value exemption or universal validation of indecency definitions follows.

**Holdings navigation:** Denver Area Educational Telecommunications Consortium, Inc. v. FCC; Alliance for Community Media v. FCC, 518 U.S. 727 (1996).

## Source: state/STANDARDS_AND_TESTS.md — Cable-channel blocking duties under the 1996 Act

### Cable-channel blocking duties under the 1996 Act

**Governing question:** The separate triggers for requested subscriber blocking and sexually oriented channel blocking.

**Current rule:** Under 47 U.S.C. §560, upon the request of a cable-service subscriber, an operator must, without charge, fully scramble or otherwise fully block the audio and video programming of each channel carrying such programming so that one not a subscriber does not receive it. The enacted channel scope has no independent indecency or sexually oriented channel trigger. Under the distinct 47 U.S.C. §561, a multichannel distributor providing sexually explicit adult or other indecent programming on a channel primarily dedicated to sexually oriented programming must fully scramble or block that channel's audio and video so a nonsubscriber to that channel or programming does not receive it. Until compliance, it must withhold that programming during FCC-determined hours when a significant number of children are likely to view it. Both define scrambling as rearranging the signal so the programming cannot be viewed or heard understandably.

**Authority by component:**

- Independent request and program-category commands, definitions and transitions: Public Law 104-104 §§504–505, adding Communications Act §§640–641; §504 effective 1996-02-08, §505 amendment effective 1996-03-09. Denver — independent 1996 statutory boundaries, 1996-06-28, preserves their separate status.

**Present operation effective:** Section 504, 1996-02-08; section 505, 1996-03-09.

**Exceptions and limits:** No FCC-selected hour, compliance fact, technical-feasibility finding, or general construction of subscription scope is established. Act §561’s facial-review provision is a different enactment from codified 47 U.S.C. §561.

**Holdings navigation:** Denver Area Educational Telecommunications Consortium, Inc. v. FCC; Alliance for Community Media v. FCC, 518 U.S. 727 (1996).

## Source: state/STANDARDS_AND_TESTS.md — Candidate financing, political-committee thresholds and membership exclusions

### Candidate financing, political-committee thresholds and membership exclusions

**Governing question:** Whether predominantly issue-oriented groups are exempt from FECA political-committee duties for otherwise covered candidate financing.

**Current rule:** Buckley and MCFL supply no categorical organization-wide major-purpose exemption for genuinely covered candidate financing. Section 431(4)(A) reaches a committee, club, association or other group receiving contributions aggregating over $1,000 in a calendar year or making expenditures aggregating over $1,000 in a calendar year, subject to actual definitions, exclusions and constitutional limits. Establish membership, covered transactions, actual candidate connection and thresholds before imposing duties. Where a contribution theory requires cooperation, consultation, concert, request or suggestion, ideological alignment, lobbying or partisan identity alone is insufficient. Independent expression retains its protection.

**Authority by component:**
- No categorical major-purpose exemption: Akins, Akins v. FEC — III. Major purpose is not a categorical exemption for otherwise covered candidate financing, Souter Part III with Stevens, O'Connor, Ginsburg and Breyer (five for both candidate-cash/conduit and broader covered candidate-financing subparts; Kennedy joins only the former), 1997-06-25.
- Distinct exclusions and reporting conditions: Akins, Akins v. FEC — IV. Membership exclusions and their distinct reporting proviso govern the remand, seven-Justice Part IV, 1997-06-25.

**Present operation effective:** Akins, 1997-06-25.

**Controlling construction:** Section 431(9)(B)(iii) excludes membership-organization or corporate communications to members, stockholders, or executive or administrative personnel when the organization is not primarily organized to influence federal nomination or election. Its separate proviso requires reporting direct costs of communications expressly advocating a clearly identified candidate's election or defeat when costs exceed $2,000 per election, except communications primarily about other subjects, under §434(a)(4)(A)(i) and, for a general election, (ii). Section 441b(b)(2) separately excludes corporate communications on any subject to stockholders and executive or administrative personnel and their families, and labor communications to members and their families; its nonpartisan registration/get-out-the-vote exclusion covers those respective recipients. Its separate-fund exclusion covers establishment, administration and solicitation for a political separate segregated fund by a corporation, labor or membership organization, cooperative or corporation without capital stock. Its loan exception concerns ordinary-course national or State bank loans under banking law and regulations. Sections 431(8)(B)(vi) and (9)(B)(v) cross-reference activity allowed by §441b without importing the $2,000 proviso.

**Open question:** Particular organizational or donor-disclosure burdens retain supported constitutional challenges. The construction supplies no automatic membership, transaction, threshold or liability finding.

**Holdings navigation:** Akins v. FEC (1997-06-25) — III. Major purpose is not a categorical exemption for otherwise covered candidate financing; Akins v. FEC (1997-06-25) — IV. Membership exclusions and their distinct reporting proviso govern the remand.

## Source: state/STANDARDS_AND_TESTS.md — Lemon test

### Lemon test

**Governing question:** How the Lemon test governs Establishment Clause claims.

**Current rule:** Lemon remains the governing Establishment Clause test, modified so that purpose, effects and entanglement are evidence within one inquiry: a specific prohibition or distinct doctrine controls when it resolves the conduct; an established permissible relationship defeats the claim on that ground; otherwise a legally sufficient unresolved allegation is decided through attribution and function under ordinary procedure. These are three routes within the updated Lemon test, not freestanding prongs, exemptions from review or an alternative test for uncertain cases. Clear cases apply that test; its former independent prongs do not remain a separate or fallback method.

**Authority by component:**

- General inquiry and operative qualifications: Agostini v. Felton — I. Lemon governs through one inquiry into governmental religious authority and function, 1997-06-23. Part I of the Opinion of the Court; Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas join this modification in full, supplying five direct joins at this level of generality.
- Object of the inquiry, evidence role and Holding I limits: Agostini v. Felton — I. Lemon governs through one inquiry into governmental religious authority and function (controlling explanation and precedent treatment), 1997-06-23; the same five Justices.
- Safeguarded secular instruction: Agostini v. Felton — II. Separately administered secular instruction is not forbidden because of its religious-school location, 1997-06-23. Part II of the Court's opinion; the five-Justice coalition stated above directly joins the rule, its secular-control qualifications and its precise treatment of precedent.

**Present operation effective:** Agostini v. Felton, June 23, 1997.

**Object of the inquiry:** Lemon addresses governmental sponsorship of religion, financing of religious functions and the improper union of civil and religious authority. Its method must identify those acts rather than condemn an independently secular service because officials supervise it to remain secular. Purpose and effects help identify the governmental function; institutional relationships show who directs it.

**Operative qualifications:** The court identifies the governmental act—an official message, organized event, exercise of supervisory power or expenditure—and the function it performs. For financing, the question is what government purchases or performs; all religious conduct of the recipient need not become government speech. Private-access authorization, religious identity and physical location are relevant in context but are not individually decisive.

Government may not organize worship as its own activity, prescribe religious profession or observance, adopt doctrine as governmental truth, solicit religious adherence through public authority, govern theology or confer civil authority on a religious basis. Denominational classifications retain their distinct strict-scrutiny rule. Independently identifiable secular work in a religious setting may be purchased with enforceable controls against devotional use and diversion. Restoration of private exercise restricted by custody or comparable governmental control remains subject to the applicable accommodation inquiry, not an automatic financing prohibition. Those distinct doctrines retain their own sources and burdens.

**Safeguarded secular instruction on religious-school premises:** The Establishment Clause permits the described supplementary Title I service for children independently selected under neutral public eligibility criteria, delivered by public employees under public supervision with public secular materials, without religious instruction, team teaching or other cooperative instructional activity with religious-school teachers, or participation in worship, and with effective controls against diversion. Religious-school location, presumed inculcation arising from that location, incidental benefit to the school, and ordinary monitoring of that separately administered public service do not by themselves establish unconstitutional sponsorship, devotional financing or institutional control; Aguilar's categorical prohibition and Ball's corresponding Shared Time rationale are displaced at that scope.

**Exceptions and limits:** Once prohibited conduct is established, actual conversion, religious success or a completed penalty is unnecessary. A secular objective cannot justify prohibited religious means. Conversely, a provider's religious motivation does not make its secular work devotional. Ordinary monitoring of secular performance is not itself establishment, even when repeated; civil control of doctrine and religious control of public authority remain forbidden.

This method requires real operational separation and reliable accountability for funded work, but not invariably separate institutional governance or wholly distinct personnel. Shared structures cannot excuse devotional financing, and they do not automatically defeat a genuinely severable public service. An institutional-support objection warrants scrutiny of the service, not a presumption that its secular and religious functions are inseparable. Neutral eligibility or public employment alone does not excuse devotional instruction or diversion; independent prohibitions on governmental worship, devotional financing and improper institutional control remain.

**Former-rule note:** Lemon's independent purpose, primary-effect and excessive-entanglement prongs ceased to govern as separate or fallback tests on June 23, 1997 (Agostini v. Felton, Holding I); those considerations now serve as evidence within the updated Lemon test. Aguilar v. Felton is overruled as authority for categorically excluding this safeguarded public Title I service from religious-school premises (Agostini v. Felton, Holding II, June 23, 1997); no reopening of completed claims or authorization of religious instruction follows. School District of Grand Rapids v. Ball loses its contrary Shared Time rationale at the same scope; its Community Education judgment and unaddressed grounds remain undisturbed.

**Open question:** Agostini v. Felton decides no tuition-voucher or provider-redemption scheme, monument, legislative-prayer program, general accommodation rule or all forms of direct aid, and does not declare every future application compatible with every preserved precedent. Parental selection does not itself answer what a particular expenditure purchases; no conclusion about an absent program displaces Witters or Mueller, whose actual holdings remain in force.

**Preserved specific holdings:** Existing specific holdings continue to govern their established relationships: Kiryas Joel restricts denominational allocation of civil power; Rosenberger permits its controlled publication benefit; Pinette and Chabad distinguish private expression and governmental attribution. Zobrest supplies no Establishment Clause merits holding.

**Related current rules:** Denominational selection for general governmental authority; Private religious viewpoints in public forums and printing benefits. Each states existing specific holdings that continue to govern their established relationships under this test.

**Holdings navigation:** Agostini v. Felton (1997-06-23) — I. Lemon governs through one inquiry into governmental religious authority and function; II. Separately administered secular instruction is not forbidden because of its religious-school location.

## Source: state/STANDARDS_AND_TESTS.md — Official graduation worship and independent religious pressure

### Official graduation worship and independent religious pressure

**Governing question:** Clergy-led prayer organized by public secondary-school officials for the official graduation program.

**Current rule:** Officials violate the Establishment Clause by selecting clergy, requesting invocations and benedictions, guiding their religious content and incorporating the worship into the official ceremony. Nonsectarian wording and optional attendance do not cure that sponsorship; no separate punishment or quantified-pressure showing is required for this ground. Independently, school-directed clergy prayer is forbidden when the school's control and the occasion's importance place objecting adolescents under substantial pressure to participate, appear to participate or forgo the ceremony. Optional attendance and standing or remaining silent do not necessarily remove that pressure; mere offense is insufficient. The official-sponsorship prohibition also applies when a school organizes, adopts, directs or religiously supervises worship as part of its own event but uses student initiation or delivery or a statutory disclaimer. Those features alone do not make the worship private, and the sponsorship ground remains independent of quantified pressure. Separately, actual pressure to participate in school-directed worship in compulsory or comparably pressured settings violates the Establishment Clause; offense at a genuinely private viewpoint alone does not suffice.

**Authority by component:**

- Independently sufficient sponsorship ground: Lee v. Weisman, “Officially organized graduation worship is forbidden”, June 24, 1992; Blackmun's Part II joined by Stone, Stevens, O'Connor and Souter.
- Independently sufficient pressure ground: *Lee*, “The school-directed ceremony also imposes impermissible religious pressure”; Kennedy's concurrence joined by Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor and Souter. Each rule has five direct supporters; neither depends on Marks or on treating Stone as joining Kennedy.

- Ingebretsen v. Jackson Public School District — II. Official school worship cannot be insulated by student delivery or a disclaimer, 1997-03-19; Ginsburg Part II with Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Souter and Breyer (six) supplies official sponsorship despite student delivery/disclaimer
- Ingebretsen v. Jackson Public School District — III. School-directed worship in a pressured setting is independently forbidden, 1997-03-19; Ginsburg Part III with Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter and Breyer (six) independently supplies actual-pressure protection

**Present operation effective:** Ingebretsen, 1997-03-19, for student-delivery/disclaimer boundaries; Lee's graduation components retain 1992-06-24 authority.

**Exceptions and limits:** Lee applied existing school-worship principles and Lemon's then-independent primary-effect prong at this recurring graduation setting without creating a replacement establishment test. Private prayer and genuinely private equal-access speech remain distinct. Adult legislative prayer, general accommodation, funding and denominational aid are not decided.

**Related current rules:** Lemon test states the governing Establishment Clause inquiry as updated in Agostini v. Felton (June 23, 1997, Holding I).

**Holdings navigation:** Lee v. Weisman, 505 U.S. 577 (1992) — Official school prayer. Ingebretsen v. Jackson Public School District (1997-03-19) — First Amendment / Official school worship and private student expression.



**Remedial limit:** Relief must address supported unlawful official worship while preserving independently lawful provisions and genuinely private expression; graduation receives no categorical exemption.

## Source: state/STANDARDS_AND_TESTS.md — Private religious viewpoints in public forums and printing benefits

### Private religious viewpoints in public forums and printing benefits

**Governing question:** Viewpoint-neutral access for private religious expression and the distinct establishment inquiry in the forums and benefits actually opened.

**Current rule:** When a public school admits private family-life and child-rearing discussion to an after-hours forum, it cannot exclude an otherwise eligible presentation solely for its religious viewpoint, even assuming a nonpublic forum. Neutral restrictions outside the forum's actual subjects remain possible. Equal access to voluntary, publicly accessible after-hours films without school sponsorship does not establish religion. That establishment holding concerns private speech, not an official devotional program; it supplies no replacement for the inherited establishment framework or universal ruling on worship restrictions. A State may not categorically exclude an otherwise eligible private unattended religious display from a forum actually opened to comparable expression merely because its message is religious or its location adjoins or lies within a capitol. Pinette’s access holding includes the proposal’s offered private-attribution safeguard; it establishes neither adequacy of a later sign nor a comprehensive establishment test. Chabad separately holds that an interior capitol location does not establish religion when actual context makes the expression clearly private. Actual selection, financing, control, adoption, preference or obscured private sponsorship can alter that conclusion; no forum label or unspecified sign decides attribution. In a generally available, privately edited student-publication program, a religious editorial viewpoint cannot exclude an otherwise eligible publication from printing support. Its secular expression purpose, broad neutral availability, independent attribution and controlled vendor payments together permit that benefit; neutrality alone does not authorize devotional financing or governmental worship.

**Authority by component:**

- A religious perspective cannot be excluded from an admitted subject: Lamb's Chapel v. Center Moriches Union Free School District — A religious perspective cannot be excluded from an admitted subject, 1993-06-07. White’s speech portion; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter (7 Justices). Direct majority support at this level of generality; no Marks synthesis.
- Equal private access does not establish religion here: Lamb's Chapel v. Center Moriches Union Free School District — Equal private access does not establish religion here, 1993-06-07. White’s establishment portion; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Souter (6 Justices). Direct majority support at this level of generality; no Marks synthesis.

- 2. Ohio’s categorical Establishment Clause defense cannot sustain this exclusion: 2. Ohio’s categorical Establishment Clause defense cannot sustain this exclusion, 1995-06-29. Stone, Parts II–III, joined by O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Breyer: seven votes for this bounded speech and access holding. Those seven do not adopt one comprehensive Establishment Clause test.
- 2. Georgia’s categorical defense cannot sustain summary judgment excluding the private religious display: 2. Georgia’s categorical defense cannot sustain summary judgment excluding the private religious display, 1995-06-29. Souter, Part II, joined by Stone, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer: eight votes for this bounded judgment and speech holding; Stevens dissents from its application.
- 3. Interior location does not itself make clearly private expression governmental religion: 3. Interior location does not itself make clearly private expression governmental religion, 1995-06-29. Souter, Part III, joined by Stone, O’Connor, Ginsburg and Breyer: five votes for this contextual rule. The additional decree-specific inquiry proposed in Part IV has only O’Connor, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer; it is not a five-Justice mandate.
- Religious viewpoint in the private publication program: Religious viewpoint in the private publication program, 1995-06-29. Kennedy’s Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, O’Connor, Scalia and Thomas; five Justices adopt the program-specific viewpoint rule.
- Permissible printing support on the actual program terms: Permissible printing support on the actual program terms, 1995-06-29. Kennedy’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, O’Connor, Scalia and Thomas join this concrete program conclusion. Lemon remains governing law; no new universal framework is adopted.

**Present operation effective:** June 29, 1995: Pinette’s bounded access holding, Chabad’s expressly subsequent contextual interior-forum holding, and Rosenberger’s independent same-day publication-benefit holding. Lamb’s Chapel retains its original school-forum components. Agostini v. Felton, June 23, 1997, for the operation of these establishment components within the Lemon test as updated in Agostini's Holding I.

**Holdings navigation:** Lamb's Chapel v. Center Moriches Union Free School District, 508 U.S. 384 (1993); 2. Ohio’s categorical Establishment Clause defense cannot sustain this exclusion; 2. Georgia’s categorical defense cannot sustain summary judgment excluding the private religious display; 3. Interior location does not itself make clearly private expression governmental religion; Religious viewpoint in the private publication program; Permissible printing support on the actual program terms; Agostini v. Felton (1997-06-23) — I. Lemon governs through one inquiry into governmental religious authority and function.

**Exceptions and limits:** No universal disclaimer duty, unconditional right to unattended displays, obligation to fund every religious activity, duty to keep a forum open or automatic immunity for nominal private sponsorship is created. Neutral identification rules may be considered; they are not ordered. The four-Justice additional attribution inquiry in Chabad is not a majority mandate. Pinette supplies no universal forum immunity or automatic attribution from location. Rosenberger's controlled vendor-funded printing benefit concerns financing, not merely facility access; religious editorial viewpoint there is not automatically purchased devotional instruction, and Rosenberger did not authorize every devotional expenditure.

**Material development:** Lamb's Chapel decided its establishment component under Lemon's then-independent purpose, primary-effect and entanglement prongs; effective June 23, 1997, those considerations serve as evidence within the Lemon test as updated in Agostini v. Felton, Holding I, not as separate or fallback prongs.

**Related current rules:** Lemon test states the governing Establishment Clause inquiry; under it Rosenberger's controlled publication benefit and Pinette's and Chabad's distinction between private expression and governmental attribution continue to govern their established relationships.

## Source: state/STANDARDS_AND_TESTS.md — Denominational selection for general governmental authority

### Denominational selection for general governmental authority

**Governing question:** When creating a special public district impermissibly allocates civic power on a denominational basis.

**Current rule:** The State may not select a religious denomination as the recipient of general governmental authority, even when lay officials formally exercise the power. Independently, an anomalous particular grant aligned with a denomination and lacking a neutral basis assuring comparable treatment violates the required governmental neutrality. Religious accommodation may preserve equal educational access but does not cure denominational allocation of civic power. Demographic religious concentration alone does not establish the violation, and neither rationale makes every special statute or every accommodation invalid.

**Authority by component:**

- Five-Justice denominational-allocation ground: Board of Education of Kiryas Joel Village School District v. Grumet — The State may not select a denomination as the recipient of general governmental authority, 1994-06-27. Souter's opinion, Part II introduction and II-A, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens and Ginsburg: five Justices. This is an independently sufficient ground. O'Connor does not join that portion, and Kennedy's separate judgment rationale is not counted as a join.
- Six-Justice particular-grant ground and accommodation limit: Board of Education of Kiryas Joel Village School District v. Grumet — The particular denominational grant lacks a neutral basis for equal treatment; Accommodation preserves educational access but does not cure this allocation, 1994-06-27. Souter's opinion, Part II-B, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor and Ginsburg: six Justices. This is an independently sufficient alternative ground; Kennedy expressly declines it. Souter's opinion, Part II-C and the implementing limits in Part III, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor and Ginsburg: six Justices. This holding rejects the accommodation defense and defines the remedy's limits; Kennedy reaches the judgment on his separate ground.

**Present operation effective:** Board of Education of Kiryas Joel Village School District v. Grumet, 1994-06-27.

**Exceptions and limits:** Kiryas Joel supplies no categorical prohibition on neutral arrangements serving religious communities.

**Related current rules:** Lemon test states the governing Establishment Clause inquiry as updated in Agostini v. Felton (June 23, 1997, Holding I); under it Kiryas Joel's denominational-allocation and accommodation holdings remain intact and continue to restrict denominational allocation of civil power.

**Holdings navigation:** The State may not select a denomination as the recipient of general governmental authority; Accommodation preserves educational access but does not cure this allocation.

## Source: state/STANDARDS_AND_TESTS.md — Genuine pedagogy and religious perspectives in graded assignments

### Genuine pedagogy and religious perspectives in graded assignments

**Governing question:** A student’s challenge to rejection of a religious research topic within a teacher-assessed curricular assignment.

**Current rule:** Teachers may enforce genuine topic-approval, research, source and academic-method requirements reasonably related to legitimate pedagogical concerns; religious conviction creates no exemption. But categorical exclusion of an otherwise permissible religious perspective, or a mistaken blanket legal ban on academic religious subjects, is not legitimate pedagogy merely because so labeled. Schools retain authority to select knowledge, method or perspective taught and to distinguish objective inquiry from testimony or devotional advocacy; no universal curricular viewpoint-neutrality rule follows. Summary judgment cannot rest on a merely possible academic justification where supported evidence permits a material inference of impermissible religious causation. The student must establish protected conduct and a substantial or motivating role; defendants may prove by a preponderance that the same decision would have occurred absent it.

**Authority by component:**

- Legitimate-pedagogy boundary and actual causal proof: I. Curricular authority must rest on genuine, legitimate pedagogy and II. The affirmative record leaves a material causal dispute, 1996-06-25; Souter’s unanimous opinion.

**Present operation effective:** Settle, 1996-06-25.

**Exceptions and limits:** Ordinary genuine factual or evaluative errors may remain within pedagogical discretion; the rule creates no classroom forum, automatic topic approval, compelled grade, independent investigative duty or conclusive liability from an impermissible factor where the same-decision defense is proved.

**Holdings navigation:** Settle v. Dickson County School Board.

## Source: state/STANDARDS_AND_TESTS.md — Official religious identification in governmental symbols

### Official religious identification in governmental symbols

**Governing question:** A government's use of religious symbolism as its continuing civic expression and present civil identifier.

**Current rule:** A municipality's adopted seal used to authenticate its communications, personnel and property is its own expression despite private design. Government violates the Establishment Clause when the whole context of its continuing civic expression identifies it with a particular faith; secular historical purpose and surrounding secular imagery alone do not cure that effect. Independently, a city may not deliberately and pervasively use a recognizable symbol of a particular faith as a permanent identifying part of its badge of present civil authority.

**Authority by component:**

- Robinson v. City of Edmond — II. The adopted municipal seal is the City's own expression, 1997-03-31; Part II, all nine, supplies attribution
- Robinson v. City of Edmond — III. Context establishes an impermissible official religious affiliation, 1997-03-31; Breyer Part III with Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Souter and Ginsburg (six) supplies the contextual-effects ground
- Robinson v. City of Edmond — IV. Making a faith's badge the City's permanent civil identifier is independently forbidden, 1997-03-31; Part IV, all nine, supplies the independent present-civil-identification prohibition
- Robinson v. City of Edmond — V. Relief must end the present official use and preserve the distinction from history, 1997-03-31; Part V, all nine, supplies the prospective-use/history distinction

**Present operation effective:** Robinson v. City of Edmond, 1997-03-31.

**Consequence or recurring remedy:** Relief must end the unconstitutional current official use, allowing a reasonable implementation period; accurate contextual preservation of history remains permissible.

**Exceptions and limits:** Attribution alone does not invalidate every government acknowledgment of religion. Private equal-access expression, historical presentation and materially different acknowledgments retain their distinctions. Standing does not extend to a general claim of offense at a distant symbol.

**Related current rules:** Lemon test states the governing Establishment Clause inquiry as updated in Agostini v. Felton (June 23, 1997, Holding I), in which purpose, effects and entanglement serve as evidence; Robinson's Part III contextual-affiliation ground was decided under Lemon's then-independent effects inquiry, and its Part IV civil-identifier prohibition is an independently sufficient ground.

**Holdings navigation:** Robinson v. City of Edmond (1997-03-31) — First Amendment / Official municipal religious identification; Robinson v. City of Edmond (1997-03-31) — Whether this continuing civic identifier establishes an impermissible religious affiliation.

## Source: state/STANDARDS_AND_TESTS.md — Neutral adjudication and withdrawal-liability rebuttal

### Neutral adjudication and withdrawal-liability rebuttal

**Governing question:** What instructions govern neutral adjudication and withdrawal-liability rebuttal?

**Current rule:** A plan sponsor's withdrawal-liability assessment and demand are enforcement acts, not adjudication; due process permits the preliminary demand when first adjudication is by a neutral arbitrator under lawful burdens. Section 1401(a)(3)(A) assigns the employer ordinary preponderance persuasion to disprove factual determinations, without extra appellate deference, clear-and-convincing proof or conclusiveness. Under (B), the employer may rebut actuarial calculations by a preponderance showing either aggregate unreasonableness of assumptions and methods considering plan experience and reasonable expectations, or significant error in application. Fraud is unnecessary. The judicial-review presumption remains separate.

**Authority by component:**

- The preliminary assessment is not an adjudication: Concrete Pipe & Products of California, Inc. v. Construction Laborers Pension Trust for Southern California — The preliminary assessment is not an adjudication, 1993-06-14. Souter’s Opinion of the Court, The preliminary assessment is not an adjudication portion; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct proposition-level majority; no Marks aggregation.
- The factual presumption assigns ordinary persuasion: Concrete Pipe & Products of California, Inc. v. Construction Laborers Pension Trust for Southern California — The factual presumption assigns ordinary persuasion, 1993-06-14. Souter’s Opinion of the Court, The factual presumption assigns ordinary persuasion portion; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter (8 Justices). Direct proposition-level majority; no Marks aggregation.
- Actuarial rebuttal retains both statutory routes: Concrete Pipe & Products of California, Inc. v. Construction Laborers Pension Trust for Southern California — Actuarial rebuttal retains both statutory routes, 1993-06-14. Souter’s Opinion of the Court, Actuarial rebuttal retains both statutory routes portion; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct proposition-level majority; no Marks aggregation.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-06-14; Concrete Pipe & Products of California, Inc. v. Construction Laborers Pension Trust for Southern California, for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** Concrete Pipe & Products of California, Inc. v. Construction Laborers Pension Trust for Southern California, 508 U.S. 602 (1993); Concrete Pipe & Products of California, Inc. v. Construction Laborers Pension Trust for Southern California, 508 U.S. 602 (1993).

## Source: state/STANDARDS_AND_TESTS.md — Hostile employment conditions without a psychological-injury prerequisite

### Hostile employment conditions without a psychological-injury prerequisite

**Governing question:** When discriminatory harassment alters employment conditions under Title VII.

**Current rule:** Sex-based harassment violates Title VII when, considering all circumstances, it is sufficiently severe or pervasive to alter employment conditions and create an environment a reasonable person would find hostile or abusive and the employee actually perceives as abusive. Serious psychological injury, treatment, diagnosis and lost productivity are not prerequisites. Relevant circumstances include frequency, severity, physical threat or humiliation rather than merely offensive utterances, and unreasonable interference with work; none is indispensable. Psychological effects may inform subjective abuse. Mere offense without the objective and subjective predicates remains insufficient. The same-sex relationship of harasser and claimant does not exclude Title VII coverage. The claimant must show that the challenged conditions were imposed because of sex through competent direct or circumstantial evidence, without an invariant requirement of sexual desire, a particular orientation or an actual opposite-sex coworker. Sexual language, contact or severity alone does not conclusively establish that causal connection. An all-male workforce is part of the context rather than categorical immunity.

**Authority by component:**

- Objective and subjective requirements, totality and absence of an injury floor: Harris v. Forklift Systems, Inc — Hostile employment conditions do not require psychological injury, 1993-11-09. O'Connor's opinion of the Court, joined in full by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, and Ginsburg; O'Connor and those eight Justices supply nine votes for the complete rule and reversal with remand. Direct unanimous authority; no fractured-opinion inference.

- Oncale v. Sundowner Offshore Services, Inc. — Same-sex harassment remains subject to Title VII, 1997-03-19; Thomas for all nine supplies same-sex coverage
- Oncale v. Sundowner Offshore Services, Inc. — Sexual desire is possible evidence, not a necessary element, 1997-03-19; Thomas for all nine supplies evidentiary possibilities and the independent because-of-sex requirement

**Present operation effective:** Oncale, 1997-03-19, for same-sex coverage and causation boundaries; Harris's environment threshold retains 1993-11-09 authority.

**Open question:** A sex-specific reasonable-person formulation and constructive discharge remain unresolved by these holdings. Employer attribution is a separate inquiry governed where applicable by Employer attribution of supervisor-created harassment.

**Holdings navigation:** Hostile employment conditions do not require psychological injury. Oncale v. Sundowner Offshore Services, Inc. (1997-03-19) — Labor and Employment / Hostile work environments.

## Source: state/STANDARDS_AND_TESTS.md — ERISA guaranteed-benefit exclusions and insurer fiduciary functions

### ERISA guaranteed-benefit exclusions and insurer fiduciary functions

**Governing question:** Which insurer assets are excluded from plan-asset status by ERISA §401(b)(2).

**Current rule:** A guaranteed-benefit policy itself is a plan asset; the insurer’s underlying assets are not deemed plan assets solely because it issued the policy only to the extent the policy genuinely guarantees the amount of benefits. A nonguaranteed component does not gain the exclusion from general-account placement. Plan-asset status subjects the insurer to §3(21)(A) fiduciary duties to the extent it exercises the specified authority or control over management or disposition; breach, causation, loss and remedy remain separate. McCarran-Ferguson does not displace ERISA’s express insurance-related duties. Compatible state insurance regulation survives the saving clause, but a concrete conflicting obligation cannot control over ERISA itself. An agency interpretation cannot erase the exclusion’s component limitation.

**Authority by component:**

- Component-specific exclusion and asset-and-function inquiry: John Hancock Mutual Life Insurance Co. v. Harris Trust & Savings Bank — The guaranteed-benefit exclusion operates by component, 1993-12-13. Ginsburg's opinion for the Court, joined by Stone, Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia, and Souter. Six Justices adopt the component-specific construction and its application to the disputed free funds.
- Federal duties and compatible state insurance regulation: John Hancock Mutual Life Insurance Co. v. Harris Trust & Savings Bank — Insurance regulation does not displace ERISA's express direction, 1993-12-13. Ginsburg's opinion for the Court, joined by Stone, Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia, and Souter. This six-Justice ground rejects the asserted state-insurance obstacle to affirmance.
- Statutory limit on the categorical agency exclusion: John Hancock Mutual Life Insurance Co. v. Harris Trust & Savings Bank — An agency interpretation cannot erase the qualified exclusion, 1993-12-13. Ginsburg's opinion for the Court, joined by Stone, Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia, and Souter. These six adopt the statutory-limit ground; the dissenters separately reject the categorical agency position without adopting the Court's entire investment-risk analysis.

**Present operation effective:** John Hancock Mutual Life Insurance Co. v. Harris Trust & Savings Bank, 1993-12-13.

**Open question:** No universal guarantee formula, illiquidity-only rule, categorical rejection of changed agency views or general rule for agency litigation positions is adopted.

**Holdings navigation:** An agency interpretation cannot erase the qualified exclusion; The guaranteed-benefit exclusion operates by component; Insurance regulation does not displace ERISA's express direction.

## Source: state/STANDARDS_AND_TESTS.md — Public-employee speech and the employer’s mistaken factual account

### Public-employee speech and the employer’s mistaken factual account

**Governing question:** Which employer beliefs about employee speech protect a disciplinary decision, and which do not.

**Current rule:** An employer acting on a genuine, reasonable account of speech that would be disciplinable under the governing public-employee-speech rules is protected even if that factual account is mistaken. Conversely, an unreasonable and incorrect account does not defeat a claim when the employee actually spoke protected speech and that speech caused the adverse action. The employee must show protected speech was a substantial or motivating factor; the employer may establish by a preponderance that it would have taken the same action absent the protected speech. These limited propositions do not impose liability for every investigation defect or adopt the plurality’s complete investigative procedure.

**Authority by component:**

- Seven-Justice genuine-and-reasonable-account safe harbor: Waters v. Churchill — A genuine, reasonable account of disciplinable speech supplies a limited safe harbor, 1994-05-31. O'Connor's plurality opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Souter and Ginsburg, adopts this sufficient condition; Scalia's concurrence in the judgment, joined by Kennedy and Thomas, expressly accepts it under their broader genuine-ground rule. Seven Justices agree at this precise level; only four adopt the full objective-inquiry framework.
- Six-Justice unreasonable-and-false-account limit: Waters v. Churchill — An unreasonable false account cannot defeat actually protected speech, 1994-05-31. O'Connor, Stone-Zsela, Souter and Ginsburg accept this proposition under objective factual care; Stevens's dissent, joined by Blackmun, expressly accepts it under their broader actual-speech rule. Six Justices agree on this proposition, but the two dissenters do not join the reasonable-error safe harbor or the judgment vacating the appellate rule.
- Motive and same-decision proof: Waters v. Churchill — The motive dispute requires remand without restoring defendants' summary judgment, 1994-05-31. O'Connor's four-Justice plurality and Scalia's three-Justice concurrence in the judgment support this common vacatur and limited remand. Blackmun and Stevens would affirm the appellate reversal under their different actual-speech rule; their votes are not counted as support for vacatur.

**Present operation effective:** Waters v. Churchill, 1994-05-31.

**Open question:** Unreasonable belief where the actual speech was unprotected, and a universal investigation checklist, are not resolved. The four-Justice complete reasonableness framework is noncontrolling.

**Holdings navigation:** A genuine, reasonable account of disciplinable speech supplies a limited safe harbor; An unreasonable false account cannot defeat actually protected speech; The motive dispute requires remand without restoring defendants' summary judgment.

## Source: state/STANDARDS_AND_TESTS.md — Former employees within Title VII retaliation protection

### Former employees within Title VII retaliation protection

**Governing question:** Whether separation alone removes a worker from §704(a)’s protected employee category.

**Current rule:** Title VII §704(a) includes former employees alleging that their former employer retaliated after separation for Title VII-protected activity. Former status alone therefore does not defeat coverage. The claimant must still establish prohibited conduct, motive, causation, actionable harm and an available remedy under their applicable requirements.

**Authority by component:**

- Robinson v. Shell Oil Co. — Robinson v. Shell Oil Co. — Former employees fall within this retaliation protection, February 18, 1997; Souter, all nine Justices.

**Present operation effective:** Robinson, February 18, 1997.

**Open question:** The decision adopts no general actionable-harm or causation standard for postemployment conduct.

**Related current rules:** Title VII employer-size counting through employment relationships uses §701(b)’s separate daily time units; a separated worker does not remain in later daily employer counts.

**Holdings navigation:** Robinson v. Shell Oil Co. (1997-02-18) — Former employees fall within this retaliation protection.

## Source: state/STANDARDS_AND_TESTS.md — Employer attribution of supervisor-created harassment

### Employer attribution of supervisor-created harassment

**Governing question:** When Title VII attributes a supervisor-created hostile environment to the employer without higher-management notice.

**Current rule:** The plaintiff must prove by a preponderance that actual delegated supervisory power materially aided creation or maintenance of an actionable hostile environment. Hiring or firing authority, alter-ego rank, higher-management notice, an express threat, completed personnel action or tangible economic loss is not invariably required; rank, employment or proximity alone is insufficient. Personal purpose or breach of a harassment prohibition does not by itself negate actual aid. Ordinary scope of employment, employer negligence and apparent authority remain separate routes. Apparent authority requires a reasonable belief traceable to the employer's manifestations and reasonable reliance on that appearance, not merely the supervisor's asserted power; it is not an additional element of actual-authority attribution. Prohibitions and correction after notice do not themselves erase earlier authority-aided conduct.

**Authority by component:**

- Andrade v. Mayfair Management, Inc. — Employer responsibility through delegated authority, 1997-02-26; Breyer for all nine supplies the agency-attribution instruction; Meritor's no-automatic-imputation and no-universal-notice-defense limits remain
- Actual-power rule and absence of automatic management-notice requirement: Faragher, Faragher v. City of Boca Raton — I. Actual delegated authority can establish responsibility without higher-management notice, Breyer Part I, all nine, 1997-09-08; Harrison, Harrison v. Eddy Potash, Inc. — I. Material aid from actual delegated power does not require alter-ego rank, Stone-Zsela Part I, all nine, 1997-09-15.
- Independent apparent-authority mechanics and policy boundary: Harrison, Harrison v. Eddy Potash, Inc. — II. The charge prejudicially withheld the supported actual-authority route and Harrison v. Eddy Potash, Inc. — III. Policy and response do not cure the omitted route; a general complete defense remains open, all nine, 1997-09-15; Faragher, Faragher v. City of Boca Raton — III. This ineffective-policy contention does not defeat liability; a general defense remains open, eight Associates, 1997-09-08.

**Present operation effective:** Andrade, February 26, 1997, for actual-authority aid; Harrison, September 15, 1997, for the express proof burden and distinct apparent-authority formulation.

**Exceptions and limits:** No general responsibility for every supervisor's private act or new affirmative defense is established. The hostile-environment elements, attribution, evidentiary sufficiency and proper remedy remain distinct.

**Related current rules:** Hostile employment conditions without a psychological-injury prerequisite states the substantive environment threshold, which does not itself establish attribution.

**Open question:** A complete defense based on genuinely effective protection and unreasonable nonuse, its elements and burden, relation to tangible employment action and any distinct remedial limitation remain undecided. No general reporting duty, policy immunity, statutory publication duty, negligence per se for failed dissemination or new burden shift follows. Discriminatory causation and the objective/subjective hostile-environment threshold remain separate from agency.

**Current wording authority:** Faragher, September 8, 1997, applies and clarifies the retained actual-aid rule without adopting a prevention/nonuse defense.

**Holdings navigation:** Andrade v. Mayfair Management, Inc. (1997-02-26) — Labor and Employment / Supervisory harassment and employer attribution; Faragher v. City of Boca Raton (1997-09-08) — Whether actual delegated authority establishes attribution without higher-management notice; Harrison v. Eddy Potash, Inc. (1997-09-15) — Labor and Employment / Supervisory harassment and employer attribution; Harrison v. Eddy Potash, Inc. (1997-09-15) — Whether the charge prejudicially excluded the supported actual-authority route; Harrison v. Eddy Potash, Inc. (1997-09-15) — Whether policy and response cure the omitted route or establish a complete defense; Faragher v. City of Boca Raton (1997-09-08) — Labor and Employment / Supervisory harassment and employer attribution.

## Source: state/STANDARDS_AND_TESTS.md — Reasonably equivalent value in a forced real-estate sale

### Reasonably equivalent value in a forced real-estate sale

**Governing question:** How §548(a)(2) measures value at a mortgage foreclosure.

**Current rule:** Compliance with state foreclosure procedures does not conclusively establish reasonably equivalent value. Compare the price with value under realistically comparable forced-sale conditions, accounting for legally and practically relevant sale constraints. Procedure is relevant evidence, but ordinary open-market appraisal alone is not the comparison and hindsight alone does not establish avoidability. The challenger retains the burden of proving the statutory avoidance predicates, including inadequate value and the independently applicable timing and financial-condition requirements. No mandatory evidence checklist, fixed percentage, categorical price floor or presumption from a low appraisal ratio replaces that inquiry.

**Authority by component:**

- Independent federal value inquiry and comparable forced-sale conditions: BFP v. Resolution Trust Corp — Regular foreclosure procedure does not conclusively establish federal value; Value is compared under realistically comparable forced-sale conditions, 1994-05-23. Souter's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens and Ginsburg; five Justices directly adopt the proposition and explanation. No fractured-decision synthesis is needed.

**Present operation effective:** BFP v. Resolution Trust Corp, 1994-05-23.

**Open question:** Tax sales, personal-property foreclosures and actual-fraud theories are not resolved. Regular foreclosure is neither a conclusive safe harbor nor automatically avoidable.

**Holdings navigation:** Regular foreclosure procedure does not conclusively establish federal value; Value is compared under realistically comparable forced-sale conditions.

## Source: state/STANDARDS_AND_TESTS.md — Judicial effect of a staged IGRA enforcement decision

### Judicial effect of a staged IGRA enforcement decision

**Governing question:** Whether a binding good-faith-negotiation adjudication becomes advisory because later mediation and executive action follow.

**Current rule:** Adjudication of IGRA's federal good-faith dispute is judicial when it conclusively determines the parties' legal relations and activates constrained statutory consequences. Later mediation and executive implementation, or absence of a guaranteed compact, do not alone make that determination advisory. The legal effect of the judgment controls; neither an immediately executable coercive decree nor assurance of all requested relief is universally necessary.

**Authority by component:**

- Judicial-function holding: Seminole Tribe — Holding I, March 27, 1996; Part I for O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer (seven).

**Present operation effective:** Seminole Tribe, March 27, 1996.

**Exceptions and limits:** A declaratory label alone supplies no controversy, substantive cause or missing legal consequence. The holding manufactures no coercive remedy or guarantee of the claimant's preferred compact.

**Holdings navigation:** Seminole Tribe of Florida v. Florida, 517 U.S. 44.

**Related current rules:** Congressional reopening of completed private federal judgments states the distinct Morgan Stanley and Plaut rule. Those decisions do not determine whether IGRA's conclusive adjudication ceases to be judicial because mediation or executive action follows.

## Source: state/STANDARDS_AND_TESTS.md — Bounded presidential narrowing of military capital punishment

### Bounded presidential narrowing of military capital punishment

**Governing question:** Presidential authority to identify narrowing factors within Congress’s military capital scheme.

**Current rule:** UCMJ Articles 18 and 56 authorize presidential restrictions on the death punishment Congress selected for Article 118(1) and (4); Article 36 supplies additional confirmation and authority for pretrial, trial and post-trial procedures, including modes of proof, for courts-martial, military commissions and other military tribunals, and courts-of-inquiry procedure. Generally recognized federal criminal-trial principles of law and evidence apply so far as the President considers practicable. Regulations may not contradict or be inconsistent with the Code, must be uniform insofar as practicable and must be reported to Congress. That authority supports RCM 1004’s narrowing restrictions without a further formula naming capital aggravators. It does not authorize new capital offenses, death under Article 118(2) or (3), or punishment contradicting or exceeding Congress’s choice. Congress may assign narrowing-factor implementation where it defines the offenses and punishment, supplies intelligible legal boundaries and leaves implementation within the President’s military-administration function. The offense selections, death-or-life alternatives, express limiting assignment and consistency restraints adequately guide this task even assuming greater criminal/capital specificity is required.
