The equality remand neither selects automatic strict scrutiny nor establishes independent liability. The ordinary fiscal classification and a penalty on protected conduct require distinct treatment. The Court preserves actual elements, defenses and relief requirements rather than treating the petition holding as judgment on every alternative constitutional theory.

**4. Personal liability and relief require their own predicates.** The case returns for lawful adjudication of causation, individual responsibility, municipal attribution, immunity, injury and remedy. The Board admission establishes its stated reason, not every individual's participation or all conditions of relief. The superintendent's favorable recommendation does not become an adverse vote. Parts I and IV have the six-Justice coalition identified above.

Crawford-El preserves competent direct and circumstantial proof under ordinary burdens. Mt. Healthy requires protected activity and a substantial or motivating role; an actual same-decision defense must be proved by a preponderance, rather than supplied by a conceivable lawful reason. Harlow and Anderson preserve particularized notice at the conduct date; Elder requires all relevant earlier authority and permits a dispositive legal defense without a mandatory merits-first sequence. For the August 16, 1993 refusal, later San Filippo, Umbehr, O'Hare, Crawford-El, Jeffries, Clanton and today's decision cannot provide retrospective notice. The Court grants or denies no ultimate personal immunity and guarantees no discovery or trial.

**5. State-claim discretion must be reconsidered on the corrected federal disposition.** Section 1367(c)(3) permits declining related state claims after all original claims are dismissed; it neither removes supplemental power nor commands nonretention. Because federal claims survive the categorical grounds used below, the Ohio claim's nonretention on that basis is vacated for lawful reconsideration. The five-Justice Part III coalition adopts this holding.

Chicago distinguishes power from discretion. The Court does not decide the Ohio claim's merits, compel retention or foreclose another valid nonretention ground whose actual predicates exist.

## Precedent Treatment

Perry and Mt. Healthy retain the entitlement/protection distinction and actual causation defense. California Motor Transport and Smith supply the petition/access principles applied to this established benefits mechanism. Pickering, Connick and Rankin retain their employee-speech rules; the Court distinguishes the present petition rather than finding public-concern speech. Umbehr and O'Hare retain their distinct contractor and affiliation holdings and causal limits. Beach, Heller and Romer remain the ordinary equality authorities within their actual scopes. Collins supplies the limited substantive-due-process analysis; Albright remains fractured. Crawford-El, Harlow, Anderson and Elder preserve ordinary proof and personal defenses. Chicago governs supplemental discretion. No precedent is overruled.

## Law After Decision

Effective January 5, 1998, this lawful unemployment-benefit invocation receives Petition Clause protection against nonrenewal imposed solely for its lawful cost without a public-concern requirement. The actual employment-petition boundary is extended to this mechanism; no universal immunity for personal grievances is adopted. The separate access and protected-right equality claims return within their stated limits. Independent economic equality and substantive due process remain rejected; individual immunity, attribution and relief are undecided.

## Separate Writings

Chief Justice Stone-Zsela joins the petition protection and count-1 remand but would affirm the remaining dispositions. His own rule protects lawful, non-sham invocation of an established administrative benefits process without requiring public-concern content, while permitting anti-fraud measures, eligibility contests, neutral filing rules, lawful prospective changes and independent job-related decisions. He considers the specific Petition Clause source sufficient without an independent substantive-due-process or equal-protection claim and would leave the other dispositions affirmed. He seeks adjudication of sole retaliatory causation and the unresolved personal defenses and remedy; he does not infer every defendant's liability from the Board's admission. His broader formulation and different component remedies are not the Court's rule.

Justice O'Connor, joined by Justices Scalia and Thomas, would affirm the federal defense judgment under the employee public-concern requirement. These applications sought personal income replacement, and their formal governmental setting does not, in their view, remove the employment dispute from that requirement. They agree with Part II's independent equality and substantive-due-process conclusions. They do not rely solely on lack of renewal entitlement, characterize successful petitioning as incapable of retaliation, presume fraud or grant government a general fiscal license to punish independently protected conduct.

## Procedure After Action

The Sixth Circuit must implement the count-specific corrections and return for appropriate district-court proceedings. The independent equality and substantive-due-process affirmances remain operative; the abandoned procedural theory stays outside the case. Personal immunity may be resolved promptly on supported conduct-date law. No liability award, compulsory trial, new hearing or automatic return to this Court is ordered.

## Source Notes

The complete Sixth Circuit opinion, concurrence, partial dissent and footnotes, 107 F.3d 1220, supply the Board admission, annual contracts, June recommendation, August 16, 1993 nonrenewal, benefit receipts, claim inventory and express substantive-only due-process limitation. The lower petition rationales differed. Original pleadings, agency files, minutes and briefs are not independently reproduced; the published recitations do not establish every person's participation or a municipal policymaking predicate. San Filippo is persuasive 1994 lower-court authority, not controlling Supreme Court law or notice for the 1993 refusal. Missing originals establish no litigant default or factual concession.
